Units are compiled without the optimiser, as Metro serves unminified dev bundles (770 -> 570 ms a save on rn-showcase). Proved: rn-showcase opened by deep link on the API 37 emulator, a scripted save of Forms.tsx refreshed in 854 ms with a ticked checkbox kept; on the iOS 26.3 simulator in 725 ms; desktop player 61-87 ms; compile 570-640 ms, device 41-51 ms. Not proved: scanning the QR on a real device over Wi-Fi (criterion 4). docs/reports/zinc-next-zinc-go.md. | Revisit if a save of a large project stays above 1 s (a parse cache, or compiling inside zinc dev's process). |
| D141 | Handheld and console targets (ZN-453 to ZN-457, 2026-10-17) | **psp, vita, n3ds, ios-legacy and ps2 share one portable core: a prebuilt interpreter core per console (the ZBC program and resources embedded or beside it), with AOT as an option for speed; psp and ps2 use f32 numbers, vita, n3ds and ios-legacy f64; no libuv on the consoles (a small system host in targets/console); one renderer per GPU over device kernels written clean-room (no Pocket3D code copied; software raster first); SDKs pinned by SHA-256 in the toolchain manager (pspdev v20261001, VitaSDK 20261015, ps2dev v2.0.0, devkitARM through a pinned docker image because pkg.devkitpro.org refuses scripts and its installer needs sudo); emulators: the owner's installs first (PPSSPP, Vita3K with its firmware, PCSX2 with the owner's BIOS, Azahar), pinned ones for other machines (PPSSPPHeadless built from source, Play! for a PS2 without BIOS); no ROM, BIOS or firmware in the repository.** Scored (RULES §4): prebuilt interpreter core 63, AOT only 57, a QuickJS core 44 (the interpreter runs numeric kernels 5.4 to 6.9 times faster than QuickJS; PocketJS holds a 60 fps PSP game with 2.2 ms of QuickJS a frame). Proved: "hello from zinc" with a moving rect and text in PPSSPP, Vita3K, PCSX2 (byte-identical frame in Play!) and Azahar (`tests/t2/console_emulators.sh`); `tools/build-console-core` and `tools/console-run`; docs/targets/handhelds.md. | Revisit when breakout misses 60 fps in PPSSPP at 333 MHz. |
| D142 | The Android GPU layer on demand (2026-10-17, examples/music on the Nexus 7) | **An Android window is the CPU's (ZN-743's zero-copy present) and becomes a GL window with the GPU layer only while frames draw a SHADER; it goes back to the CPU once frames without a shader rest (no damage), or after 60 of them. The GL context, programs and textures are kept; the CPU's hold on the buffer queue is released with ANativeWindow `perform(NATIVE_WINDOW_API_DISCONNECT, NATIVE_WINDOW_API_CPU)`; if that fails, `shaderAvailable()` becomes false and the program draws its CPU path. On the GL window a frame is rendered once and only the rectangles drawn over a shader are rendered again for their alpha (stored inverted in the alpha byte, uploaded with no conversion). Bilinear images are sampled by rows (each source row blended once and reused), with a NEON path for opaque pictures on ARM. `zinc export --apk --release` builds a release APK; a release build reads only ZINC_PRESENT_STATS and ZINC_GL_STATS from zinc.env in /sdcard/Android/data/<id>/files/.** Why: the music remote ran its whole session on the GL window because the lava face makes its shader at load, so every damaged frame was rendered twice, converted on one core (30 ms) and uploaded (11 ms); the player's 12x12 stretched backdrop took 77% of the raster (107 ns a pixel, now 53). Measured on the Nexus 7, release APKs: carousel (no shader) p50 183 -> 50 ms, into and out of the lava 183 -> 67-83 ms, radios scroll 67 ms with 467-650 ms hitches -> 33 ms flat, the lava face at rest 60 fps both. Rejected: the shader on the CPU only (lava at 22 fps), a GL window for the whole run, hiding the SurfaceView to switch (a visible flash), two textures uploaded in turn (11-12 ms either way on Tegra 3). | Revisit when an Android device refuses the disconnect, when the ~380 ms switch back (mostly the compositor) is measured with systrace, or with a compositor-moved layer for the carousel. |
| D143 | zinc:link: two Zinc programs that find each other, pair with a code and talk encrypted (ZN-756, owner 2026-10-17) | **plugins/link speaks zinc agent's protocol: DNS-SD `_zinclink._tcp` (TXT id/svc/name/pair/v), a clear line `ZNL1 pair\|sess`, TLS 1.2 EC J-PAKE with the 6-digit code (5 minutes, 3 tries, one peer), then TLS 1.3 PSK+ECDHE with the paired key; JSON and byte frames, pings after 15 s of silence, reconnects (1 to 30 s) while paired. The DNS-SD and frame code moved from src/agent to src/net (shared, not copied); the plugin compiles tls.cpp in its own namespace (`ZN_TLS_NS`) so it never clashes with the engine's copy. Native side on the program's thread with non-blocking sockets, events pulled every 10 ms (mbedTLS is never entered from two threads). Android discovery: a WifiManager.MulticastLock held from onResume to onPause with the shared C++ responder; keys in `$ZINC_HOME/link/<app>.keys`, mode 0600.** Scored: MulticastLock + the shared responder 69 against NsdManager 50 (a second implementation in Java, and Android 7's NSD resolves one service at a time); a 0600 plugin file rather than zinc:storage (storage.kv is shared by every program on a desktop and written 0644). Measured Mac <-> Nexus 7: found in 63 ms, a wrong code refused, paired (J-PAKE ~0.4 s on the Tegra 3), JSON and bytes both ways, a restart reopened the session with the stored key, a restart under an open channel reconnected in 4 s. WebRTC across networks is planned (docs/plugins/link.md, "Across networks": the same frames over a data channel, a signalling room named by a hash of the code then of the key, the J-PAKE/PSK TLS inside the data channel), not built. | Revisit for discovery with the app in the background, the system keychains for keys, or when WebRTC lands. |
| D144 | 3D on the consoles: each console's GPU, existing code, Pocket3D's cooking (owner, 2026-10-17) | **zinc:3d draws on each console's GPU through the SDKs and proven code of docs/reports/games/console-2d-3d-frameworks.md, never a new software 3D: PSP sceGu/sceGum hardware T&L with VFPU matrices (ZN-491 kernel, ZN-476 zinc:3d), Vita GXM with precompiled GXP from Cg (ZN-493, ZN-477; no vitaGL: LGPL-3.0 and libshacccg extracted by the user), 3DS citro3d with picasso programs and stereo from the 3D slider (ZN-495, ZN-478), PS2 gsKit triangles fed by render3d's CPU front half, then AthenaEnv's MIT VU1 microprograms (ZN-760). Pocket3D's method for assets: one source, a cooker driven by a per-console profile run by `zinc build --target <console>` (quantized chunked meshes with baked light ZN-489, swizzled/tiled/CLUT/ETC1 textures ZN-452, pack viewer ZN-490), budgets are build errors, every cook writes a deterministic receipt. Order: ZN-759's hardware demos first, then the GPU back ends, audio (ZN-482, no longer waiting on the iPhone 4S), and marble last (ZN-754 depends on them all); then the owner runs everything on the real consoles they own (ZN-754 AC4).** The chain is handed to agents by the coordinator as the owner's request; the cross-platform group of priority.json is unchanged for the rest of m-23. | Revisit if a console's GPU path fails its frame budget in the emulator, or on the owner's hardware results. For the Vita, "no vitaGL" is superseded by D165 (vitaGL for zinc:gl, WebGL 1, three.js and zinc:3d; GXM stays the 2D default). |
| D145 | The web target (2026-10-17, `zinc-web-export.md`, `docs/targets/web.md`) | **The app's `zinc bundle` JavaScript runs on the browser's own engine behind a thin JS host (`targets/web/runtime.js`); no ZBC, no AOT, no wasm for ordinary apps (scored 77 against 54 interpreter in wasm, 50 AOT to wasm, 47 QuickJS in wasm; the `wasm` target stays for console-style programs). Two render modes, canvas (default, 60) and DOM (57), Zinc's classic layout does the layout in both. Hot reload is Vite-style (unbundled modules, forwarded function exports; a module that exports state reloads the page). Plugins declare `web` in plugin.json as `entry`, `native`+`impl` or `wasm` (wasm only where native code is needed: sqlite). Browser limits: `targets/web/browsers.json` (MDN), warnings W0601 to W0603 served by `webExplain`, `zinc:browser` at run time (`ZnUnsupported`).** `targets.web` holds export settings; the `web` key of zinc.json stays the webenv switch. Only Chrome was run. |
| D146 | Semantic DOM, native video and lottie on the web (2026-10-17, `zinc-web-export.md` "Decision 2b") | **`--render dom` is a real DOM laid out by the browser's CSS: atomic generated classes (hashed, deduplicated), CSS custom properties for values that change, Zinc's classic layout kept underneath only for geometry calls and virtual lists (70 against 56 for the absolute painter, now deleted, 63 for a second React DOM UI, 62 for switching Zinc's layout off). Tags and roles come from node attributes (`as`, `role`, `href`...) so one source runs on every target; `Form` is a real `<form>` only in the page. Video is the browser's `<video>` with a visible fallback and export warning W0604; lottie is vendored lottie-web 5.13.0 (MIT) loaded lazily through the new plugin.json `web.scripts`. Prerender is an optional Chrome capture at export, adopted at hydration.** Chrome only so far. |
| D147 | npm packages and Vite on the web target (2026-10-17, `docs/targets/web.md`, `web-libraries.md`) | **esbuild is the single resolver: dev pre-bundles bare imports once into `/__deps` (Vite's model, cache `.zinc/web-deps/<key>` keyed by lockfile, package.json, each package's package.json and imported names), export and the QuickJS link share `webResolveFlags` (a C++ resolver was rejected: it ignored `exports` conditions and the `browser` field). The package manager is the project's own (packageManager field, then lockfile, then npm; `zinc add npm:`, `zinc install`), Zinc never installs one; Node is needed only to install (P0001, P0002, W0605). `@zinc/vite` delegates Zinc's transform to `zinc dev --prepare` (the JSX lowering needs the whole module graph) and Vite owns packages, HMR and bundling.** 13 libraries tested in dev and export; yarn and Rspack not run. |
| D148 | Web plugins: WebGL, three.js, shaders, hardware APIs (2026-10-17, `zinc-web-export.md` Decision 5) | **A WebGL picture reaches the Zinc surface as a gfx image, so it keeps paint order, clip, transform and fade and works in DOM mode (81 against 66 and 55 for a second canvas layer); the zinc:gl rows are generated from the engine's table (84); the real three.js r186 runs on a page-made WebGL2 canvas behind a thin renderer wrapper (83 against 54 for re-implementing the Zinc subset on WebGL). `drawShader` renders the GLSL ES 1.00 fragment through WebGL into an image. No Yoga and no wasm for layout on the web (owner): an explicit `ui.layout: "rn"` falls back to the classic layout with React Native semantics, `capabilities.json` `web.ui.rn: false`. physics2d is Box2D 3.1.1 in wasm through a C shim (the one native-code case). Serial, MIDI, gphoto2 (about 300 lines of PTP over WebUSB, 79 against 63 for libgphoto2 in wasm) and OSC (over a WebSocket relay) answer their native spec in JS; the map is vendored MapLibre GL JS (81 against 50 for our own renderer), loaded byte for byte.** Hardware ran against mocks only. |
| D149 | Zinc Relay: how a Mac's notifications are read (ZN-757, owner 2026-10-17) | **Notification Center's SQLite database (macOS 15: `~/Library/Group Containers/group.com.apple.usernoted/db2/db`; the schema from usernoted's own strings), read-only in place through zinc:sqlite, polled every second, under Full Disk Access granted once through the relay's setup assistant. Focus from `~/Library/DoNotDisturb/DB/Assertions.json` + `ModeConfigurations.json`; "Suivre le mode" uses the record's `presented` flag. Avatars: the record's own image, first the communication context (`req.uncc`, an NSKeyedArchiver of UNNotificationCommunicationContext): its sender's image is an `intents-remote-image-proxy:` URL that the Intents image service (private `INRemoteImageProxy _retrieveImageDataWithReply:`, guarded) returns to an unentitled process (Discord, checked on the owner's Mac 2026-10-17), and its sender's Contacts identifier gives the Contacts photo (Messages); then Messages chat.db + Contacts, then Contacts by name, then an initials monogram. Bundle ids are compared without case (Notification Center records Messages as `com.apple.mobilesms`). Login item through SMAppService, a LaunchAgent as fallback. Private APIs allowed by the owner (not on the App Store): only the Intents image proxy; the record plist is read raw.** Rejected: banners through Accessibility (on-screen only, no app id, a private view tree Apple changes); INFocusStatusCenter (needs an entitlement, yes/no only). | Revisit when a real record shows the avatar elsewhere (`--inspect`), or if a macOS release moves the database. |
| D150 | Console hardware as zinc APIs, console audio and asset packs (ZN-759, owner 2026-10-17) | **Each console's hardware is a set of zinc:sys `device()` queries (D122's pattern): the console HAL answers first (a new optional `hal_device`), then a simulated device (`zinc run --simulate psp|vita|ps2|n3ds`, `devices/*.json`), then the desktop; `zinc:hardware` wraps them (battery, network, motion, rear touch, microphone level, stereo 3D, vibration, save location). The pad is zinc:gfx gamepad 0 (D101) with pressure in `HalGamepad.value[17]` and `gamepadRumble`; the 3DS bottom screen is the program's first `openWindow`; the PS2 memory card is zinc:storage. zinc:audio on the consoles is our own small mixer exposing the same native module (WAV, Ogg through the stb_vorbis plugins/audio vendors; 44.1 kHz PSP, 48 kHz Vita and PS2, 22.05 kHz 3DS), mixed on the program's thread into a ring. Console cores link `zinc pack`'s default asset pack and serve zinc:assets and pack images from it. `Date` computes in f64 and avoids `Math.*` (32-bit under the psp/ps2 profiles).** Rejected: miniaudio on the consoles (atomics the PSP and PS2 lack, no backends for them). | Revisit with asset groups on consoles, or when a console needs streaming audio. |
| D151 | The Vita draws zinc:gfx with its GPU (ZN-470/471, owner 2026-10-17: "pourquoi la vita utilise pas son gpu… go le faire !") | **Our own GXM back end (targets/vita/gxm_vita.cpp) with SDL3's precompiled GXP programs (zlib, from the vendored SDL3): the command lists are replayed at 960x544 native (clears, square rectangles, images from atlases with flips, tint, alpha and filtering, text, rectangular clips cut on the CPU); textures and the glyph atlas in CDRAM, three display buffers flipped at vblank. A frame holding a command the replay cannot draw yet (rounded corners, gradients, borders, shadows, paths, rotated matrices) falls back to the software raster into a texture the GPU shows; `ZINC_VITA_GPU=0` keeps the old path.** Scored in docs/reports/games/console-2d-3d-frameworks.md 4.3: own GXM 59, vitaGL 50 (LGPL-3.0 and libshacccg.suprx extracted by each user), PVR_PSP2 out (Sony modules without a licence). Measured on the owner's PS Vita: Meadow Run at 60 fps, no missed vblank in 4,200 frames, work p50 3.2-3.7 ms, replay 0.3-0.44 ms (the software raster at 960x544 costs 28 ms a frame on the Mac's clock in Vita3K; the owner found it unplayable). | Revisit when a zinc:gl path on the Vita needs shaders of its own (Cg compiled on a console), or when UI kits need the rounded and gradient commands on the GPU (ZN-471). |
| D152 | Web target in a real browser (2026-10-17, `docs/targets/web-demos.md`) | **The web is tested with real Chrome and trusted input at Retina scale (`tools/web-real-test`, `--force-device-scale-factor=2`, `Input.dispatch*`), not only headless DPR 1 with synthetic events; the matrix of demos x modes is in `web-demos.md`. A raw-gfx program in DOM mode gets a lazily mounted full-window canvas (the semantic DOM has no node for it). `exit()` on the web stops the loop and throws a silent `ZnExit`. An error repeating within one second is counted, not re-reported. A component unmounted before a queued update never re-renders (shared with the desktop engine: `dead` flag in `lib/std/react.ts`).** Bugs fixed: blank screen after any resize, doubled typing in canvas text fields, blank DOM for gfx-only programs, audio never unlocked in DOM mode, favicon 404, error flood. |
| D153 | Device information (ZN-761, owner 2026-10-18) | **The expo-device API ships as the std module `zinc:device-info` over one JSON record, `device('device')` of zinc:sys (type, brand, manufacturer, modelName, modelId, designName, osName, osVersion, osBuildId, totalMemory, cpuArchitectures, isDevice); `Platform.isPad`, `isTV` and `constants` come from it.** `zinc:device` is taken by plugins/device (board services) and a plugin wins module resolution: renaming the plugin would break the board demos. Unknown fields are '' or 0, never null (typed Zinc has no null strings here) and never guessed. `getDeviceTypeAsync` is an already-settled async function (Zinc refuses `await` on a non-promise). iOS model names come from a data-only C table (`src/host/apple_models.inc`); an unknown identifier keeps its modelId with an empty name. Phone or tablet: iOS's userInterfaceIdiom; Android's UI_MODE_TYPE_TELEVISION, else smallest width 600 dp. | A program needs deviceYearClass, or Apple adds an API that names the model. |
| D154 | A UI app's memory on macOS (ZN-764, owner 2026-10-20: "moins de 100 Mo ... surtout les UI") | **The macOS window shows the software raster through Metal by default (hal_metal.mm, iOS's present since ZN-627: the frame is a shared MTLBuffer the GPU blits into a CAMetalLayer drawable, at most two drawables); OpenGL replay only with ZINC_RENDERER=gl or --simulate (it turns the picture). The desktop GL window frees the splash's rows and texture once the replay draws.** Why: a window's footprint is its window-sized buffers, 13.7 MB each at 1180x760 density 2; GL held nine (examples/agents 216 MB), Metal three plus WindowServer's (81 MB); ui/forms 133 -> 45 MB. The raster keeps 60 fps for breakout and hero on the Mac. Lost by default: zinc:gfx shaders on the GPU (music, music-tree draw their CPU picture) and GPU strokes. | A program that needs the replay's speed at Retina (a full-window animation slower than a frame on the raster), or the IOSurface-contents present (later.md) lands. |
| D155 | Loro in Zinc (owner 2026-10-21: "utiliser Loro dans zinc en natif") | **`zinc:loro` (also `loro-crdt`) wraps the Rust crate `loro` behind an `extern "C"` API built by cargo from `plugin.json` (`targets.<id>.cargo`, `--locked`, cached under ZINC_HOME); its TypeScript mirrors loro-crdt.** Containers are addressed by their id string and values cross the C boundary as JSON, numbers as doubles (loro-crdt's value semantics, a small API). Events are queued in Rust and delivered right after the call that caused them (loro-crdt delivers synchronously). A Loro panic is caught (`catch_unwind`) and becomes a `LoroError`. Rust is the user's cargo, not a pinned toolchain (pinning rustup is later.md). `setPeerId` takes a number (exact below 2^53), `setPeerIdStr` any u64: no `number \| string` parameter in the engine. Cost: +5.0 MB to an AOT program that imports it. | Loro ships a C API of its own, or Zinc gets generic instance methods (the typed containers). |
| D156 | The interface of a microcontroller (owner 2026-10-22: "il faut une version ultra light dédié pour ce genre de device à la manière de lvgl quitte d'ailleurs à l'utiliser") | **On a board without PSRAM (ESP32 under mquickjs) the interface is LVGL 9.6 itself, vendored as the plugin `zinc:lvgl` on the native ABI: one C file serves the computer's library and the firmware. LVGL keeps its objects, flex layout, scrolling, animations and drawing in C; the program holds i32 handles and polls an event queue (no C calls into JavaScript). It renders 32-row RGB565 areas into two DMA buffers that the firmware sends to the panel (`zn_panel_flush`, main/zn.h); the display list is set aside while it does. LVGL's clock is the frame's `dt`, so a deterministic run gives the same pixels. On the computer the same program shows LVGL's picture as one zinc:gfx image (`display.ts`, `display.esp32.ts` by platform suffix).** Why: zinc:ui did not fit the 96 KB JavaScript heap ("not enough memory"; 690 KB of bytecode for the widgets tour), and the owner found the zinc:gfx tour's scrolling not smooth and its frame rate low. LVGL's tour is 60 KB of bytecode, ran at 60 fps on the board (test program, first full screen in about 67 ms, idle turns 70 µs) and draws only what changed. Rejected: our own retained widget layer in C (a second LVGL to write and keep), zinc:ui trimmed for the board (the reconciler and Yoga stay too big). | Revisit when a board with PSRAM (ESP32-S3) can hold zinc:ui, or when LVGL's API grows past what handles and polling express (custom draw callbacks). |
| D157 | zinc:lvgl's memory on a board without PSRAM, and on the computer that stands for it (2026-10-22, the owner: "est-ce que le simulateur mac peut avoir des contraintes qui simulent le hardware en terme de place et ram ?") | **On the board LVGL takes the heap (`LV_STDLIB_CLIB`), shared with the firmware; on the computer (the window and the mquickjs check) it draws from LVGL's own pool of 96 KB, the board's 138 KB in 64-bit objects, so a screen that does not fit the board fails on the computer too, with "LVGL's memory pool is full". A program builds a page when it is shown and deletes it when it is left (`clean`, which also drops the handlers and queued events of the objects deleted); the firmware's main task gets a 28 KB stack when zinc:lvgl is in it.** Why: a fixed pool on the board crashed at boot (no 110 KB block left once the JavaScript heap was taken), the six pages of esp32-widgets built at once left 0 KB free in esp32sim, one page at a time leaves 37 to 102 KB (Lottie the heaviest), and ThorVG goes 21 KB deep where the default stack is 8 KB (heap corruption, then an assert). The 96 KB pool gives the same share per page as the board's heap (Lottie: 75% here, 71% there). Rejected: the pool on the board (fragments, and a second limit beside the heap), every page kept built (does not fit), LVGL's software rotation for a turned display (a second band buffer and a copy per area: the panel's MADCTL turns it for free). | Revisit when the ratio between the 64-bit pool and the board's heap drifts (a page of many small objects is cheaper on the computer: Bench 0.86, Home 0.60), or when ThorVG's `new` (outside the pool) is routed through `lv_malloc`. |
| D158 | The ESP32 emulator with touch and a live window (2026-10-22, the owner's question "comment on teste ?" once the board was away) | **esp32sim is built from its pinned v0.3.0 sources (GitHub archive, SHA-256 `9cadce5d…2533313`) with `tools/esp32sim/esp32sim-0.3.0-zinc.patch` (the ESP32-2432S022 board on the SPI wiring, the CST820 touch at 0x15, the `--web 0` port line), by the user's cargo, once, into `~/.zinc/toolchains/esp32sim-<host>-0.3.0-zinc`, as D155 does for Loro; the patch's SHA-256 is the rebuild key and `ZINC_ESP32SIM` overrides it. `--live` shows the emulator in the engine's own window, fed by esp32sim's `--web` WebSocket (RGB565 frames converted in C++), the mouse going back as touches.** Why: the release binaries lack our board and its touch controller; the engine's window inherits `ZINC_BACKGROUND`, HiDPI and closing, and needs no protocol patch. Rejected: a zinc:gfx viewer program (a compile per run, zinc:socket and its permission, a TypeScript loop over 76,800 pixels a frame), a custom pipe protocol (a bigger patch), a browser page (a window outside Zinc). | Revisit when upstream esp32sim ships the board (drop the patch, pin that release) or models the 8-bit i80 panel. |
| D159 | The languages of Zinc's tooling (owner 2026-10-22: "on gère ce onboard en multi langue donc avec des fichiers de trads et gérer anglais, français, espagnol, italien, allemand"; research `docs/reports/onboarding-i18n-research.md`) | **Flat JSON catalogs, one file per language and area: `i18n/<lang>/<area>.json` (areas `cli`, `diag`, `atelier`; every key starts with its area, `cli.new.created`), values with named `{placeholders}` and CLDR plural suffixes (`_one`, `_many`, `_other`); en, fr, es, it, de ship together. The C++ reads them with the vendored yyjson, Zinc with `JSON.parse`: no new parser, no gettext, ICU or Fluent (scored 67 against 52, 46 and 40). English is the source and is in the binary, so a missing file or key falls back to it, never to an empty string.** Language: `--lang`, then `ZINC_LANG`, then `lang` in `$ZINC_HOME/config.json` (written by `zinc config set lang`, the first-run welcome and Atelier's setting), then the system (`LANGUAGE`, `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES`, `LANG`, macOS preferred languages), then English; `fr-CA` falls back to `fr`. Never `setlocale()`. Codes (`Z0103`), command names, flags, JSON, LSP and logs stay English; `tests/run` pins `ZINC_LANG=en`. Register: vous (fr), tú (es), tu (it), du (de), fixed with the shared terms in `i18n/glossary.md`. `tools/i18n-check` (T0): same keys and placeholders in every language, plurals `one` and `other` at least, codes and backticked text unchanged, every id used in the sources present in English. Diagnostics: titles, why and fix of the registry and the frequent details; the rest stays English (rustc's stalled attempt). Files are plain JSON, so a hosted tool (Weblate) can be added later without changing them. |
| D160 | What the onboarding CLI translates and how it greets (ZN-774.01-03, 2026-10-22) | **The catalogs hold what a beginner reads: the help summaries and examples, `zinc new` (its question, list and next steps), the first-run welcome, `zinc doctor`'s short report, the run/check/dev lines; the long `zinc help <cmd>` details, `zinc doctor --details`, the self-update texts and the `zinc dev` status markers (`vN started`, `refreshed`, which tests wait on) stay English.** The welcome's marker is a key of `$ZINC_HOME/config.json` beside `lang`; the installers end with the hidden `zinc --next-steps`, so the three install paths print one text; `zinc new` with no argument asks only on a terminal and otherwise exits 2 without writing; `zinc doctor` exits 1 when the engine files are missing; the first plugin build is announced as a step line (hidden by `-q` and JSON logs). |
| D161 | The AS3/Haxe style as a module (ZN-774.13, owner 2026-10-22: "le mode processing très procédural, le mode AS3/Haxe, le mode Canvas, le mode JSX, comment on fait ?") | **`zinc:stage` (lib/std/stage.ts) is music-tree's display tree promoted to a std module: `Stage`, `DisplayObject` (alias `Sprite`), the row/column layout, the hooks (`onAddedToStage`, `onRemovedFromStage`, `onUpdate`, `onMeasure`, `onDraw`), tap and drag zones, scroll boxes, plus the AS3 names that cost nothing (`numChildren`, `getChildAt`, `addChildAt`, `contains`, `stage`, local `mouseX`/`mouseY`); no event system (nodes override hooks and register zones).** Touch zones are pooled across frames and a gesture copies the zone it pressed (`copyFrom`), so a drawn frame allocates nothing; `Stage.frame(dt)` draws only when something changed. The four styles of writing a program are: drawing (zinc:gfx), scene (zinc:stage), Canvas (zinc:canvas), interface (zinc:ui); `docs/start/styles.md` (ZN-774.14) presents them. |
| D162 | Translated diagnostics, the tour, and the compile cache (ZN-774.04, .05, .16, 2026-10-22) | **The registry `include/zn/diagnostics.h` stays the English source of every code; `i18n/en/diag.json` repeats it and `tests/t0/diagnostics.sh` keeps the two equal (`zinc explain --catalog`). The machine part of a diagnostic line (`file:line:col: error Z0103: `), `--json`, `zinc lint` and the LSP stay English in every language: Atelier and tools parse them.** `zinc tour` makes its project in the current folder through the `zinc new` question (`--auto`: `./zinc-tour`); its steps 7 and 8 (docs, Atelier) count as done once shown when nothing can be checked. The compile cache stays whole-program (generics are instantiated where used, the optimiser inlines across modules, one string and class table per ZBC): a per-module cache waits for separate compilation of lib/std; `zinc dev` now caches its hot unit, and the key gained the engine root. |
| D163 | What zinc:ui's primitives draw by default, and what a program that uses no classes compiles (ZN-774.18, owner 2026-10-22: "le zinc:ui est pas trop figé et trop lourd ? ... allègement et plus souple, moins figé graphiquement à la manière de ce que propose react-native"; report `docs/reports/ui-primitives-like-react-native.md`) | **The primitives draw nothing a program did not ask for, as React Native's: Button is a focusable box that centres its content (no fill, no padding); Input and TextArea show their text, caret and placeholder only (no fill, border, radius or padding; colour and size inherited; the 2 px focus border only on a field that has a border); Text is black on a light window, white on a dark one, black under the react-native preset. Kept: the pressed dim in the Solid/React model (zinc:react-native's Pressable shows none), the keyboard focus ring, the scroll bars, each reachable as a style key too. A codemod, `zinc migrate ui-explicit-looks`, writes the old looks into the programs that relied on them before the defaults change, so no example or proto golden moves. Every class-only visual family gets a style key (zIndex, fontStyle, textDecorationLine, textTransform, text shadow, colour alpha, weights, pointerEvents, cursor, outline, placeholder and selection colours, scroll indicators, transitions). The class parser is compiled only into programs that use classes, through a hook `setClass` installs, not a module split.** Scored E 60 (neutral + keys + hook), A 54, C (split the module) 54, D (keys only) 45, B (a preset switch) 41. Measured at frame 30 on 44 UI projects and 19 proto rows: the Button fill and the white text move nothing, the field look 4 programs, the Button's padding 2 and centring 6 (kept). Revisit if a kit or a platform look needs a default the primitives no longer give, the codemod meets dynamic classes it cannot rewrite in user projects, or splitting the module becomes cheaper than the hooks. |
| D164 | Independent Pi player bundle and engine updates (ZN-777, owner 2026-10-23) | **Keep the player's source tar unchanged on the image; repack it to `.zapp` with the selected signed engine on the Pi, then activate the tested engine/bundle pair through one atomic generation link.** A `.zapp` holds ZBC and an engine version, so one compiled bundle cannot promise arbitrary downgrades. A root-owned Python/systemd helper reuses `zinc verify --key` (Ed25519), checks SHA-256 and userspace architecture, compiles/tests as `zinc`, waits for a first-frame marker and restores the old pair on failure. Previous pair is kept for offline rollback; app data and pairing stay outside. RULES §4 totals: helper using existing verifier 60 (5,4,4,4,5,3,4), new engine updater API 57 (4,5,4,3,5,4,2), copying installs through selfupdate 53 (3,4,4,3,5,4,4). Release listing: [GitHub releases API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases). Proof and hardware limits: `image/README.md`, `pi_image_update`, `pi_image_updates`. | Revisit for independent player-source updates or signing-key rotation; real Pi power-loss/boot/display/network recovery belongs to the cross-platform pass. |
| D165 | vitaGL as the Vita's second GPU path (owner, 2026-10-24: "tu me fais un support avec vitaGL pour la psvita ?") | **The owner's request reopens D144/D151 for the 2D replay: vitaGL (third_party/vitagl, pinned, unmodified, with vitaShaRK, math-neon, SceShaccCgExt and taiHEN's stubs) is an opt-in build of the Vita core (`--vitagl`, `ZN_VITA_GL`), behind the same three entry points as the GXM back end (`targets/vita/gl_vita.cpp`), so the HAL does not change. It replays zinc:gfx with the shared GLES 2 replay (`runtime/gl_replay.cpp`, D119's base), which draws every command on the GPU where the GXM replay falls back to software. GXM stays the default: it needs nothing from the user, vitaGL needs `ur0:data/libshacccg.suprx` extracted from each console (without it the core uses the software raster) and makes the core an LGPL-3.0 combined work (relinkable objects shipped with a release).** Measured on the owner's PS Vita (gl-check, every scene): 60 fps, 0 missed vblanks, no command on the CPU; the 17 programs compile in 10.3 s at the first launch, 86 ms from vitaGL's shader cache after. Meadow Run on the same console (owner, 2026-10-25), one 600-frame window: vitaGL 60.9 fps but 29 missed vblanks, replay CPU p50 1.10 ms p99 16.1 ms, frame p99 34.2 ms; GXM 0 missed, replay p50 0.32 p99 0.39 ms. So GXM stays the 2D default and is extended (rounded, borders, shadows through a coverage atlas as the PSP's GE replay does, then gradients); vitaGL is the path for zinc:gl/WebGL1, three.js and zinc:3d (the only way to compile GLSL at run time), `--vitagl` stays for 2D. Suspected cost of vitaGL: the replay refills its vertex buffers each batch and vitaGL waits for the GPU on reuse (not measured). Not done here: zinc:gl/WebGL1 and zinc:3d on vitaGL (ZN-477), precompiled GXP for the replay's programs through `glShaderBinary` (would drop the libshacccg requirement). | Revisit when the replay's programs ship precompiled (then vitaGL could become the default), or when a hardware measure shows it slower than GXM on Meadow Run. |
| D166 | Large static worlds in zinc:3d (owner 2026-10-25: "on veut la même qualité que pocket3D aucun compromis", "une suite d'outils utilisable pour n'importe quel projet 3D") | **A static world is a `.zworld` index plus `.zwc` chunks (`include/zn/zworld.h`): cells with near, mid and far levels, each batch's indices grouped by cell and by 16 compass wall sectors plus one group for the rest, 20-byte quantised vertices that each console renderer decodes to its own layout through `worldMesh`. zinc:3d's `World` chooses natively what a frame draws (a port of Pocket Tokyo's `view.rs`: frustum, level by distance with hysteresis, no holes, facing sectors, a triangle governor) and every renderer draws index ranges. `zinc assets build` makes a world from any glTF (`"as": "world"`, cgltf and meshoptimizer's simplifier, a provenance file `<stem>.zworld.json` for the Inspector and the Blender scene contract's `worldSources`). The per-machine defaults (reaches, budget, haze, fps, night, dome, chunk idle and read time) live in `defaults3D()` of plugins/3d/index.ts, by profile name at run time, not in targets/capabilities.json.** Why: Pocket Tokyo reaches its counts with index ranges of a few big buffers and native selection, not with a scene of nodes (docs/reports/pocket3d-techniques.md); the defaults must be chosen at run time (tokyo's test runs the PSP's numbers on the Mac) and on every engine without a JSON parser on the consoles. Measured in the emulators (docs/reports/tokyo-per-device.md): PSP 38,900 triangles in 1,451 draws at 30 fps, 3DS 55,500 with shadows, ETC1 and dusk at 30 fps, Vita 30,200 at 59 fps (CPU lighting). | Revisit when zinc.json needs `"3d"` overrides, a world outgrows one far chunk, or the Vita's vertex programs (gxpc on the console) land. |

| D167 | Skeletal animation for Blender-authored Zinc scenes (ZN-830, owner workflow request 2026-10-25) | **Use pinned MIT ozz-animation 0.17.0 for skeleton/clip conversion, sampling and blending behind the zinc:animation native module; cgltf remains the model reader, and Zinc owns scene integration, deformable mesh uploads, event policy and gameplay state.** The default static glTF loader does not silently acquire animation behavior. Weights, bind pose and interpolation are validated; CPU deformation updates persistent software/GL meshes, and optional scene factories prepare a candidate skin before replacing a live revision. Decision scores in RULES order (fit, performance, footprint, maintainability, licence, portability, effort): ozz 5,4,4,4,5,4,4 = **61**; embedding the full Godot runtime for every Zinc character 3,4,1,3,5,2,2 = 43; writing our own animation mathematics 4,3,5,1,5,4,1 = 48. These are qualitative architectural scores, not timing or footprint measurements. [ozz features](https://guillaumeblanc.github.io/ozz-animation/documentation/features/) and [scope/licence](https://guillaumeblanc.github.io/ozz-animation/documentation/); vendored checksum/licence in third_party/ozz-animation/PIN and LICENSE.md. Actual animation_pipeline/animation_lab and animated_scene regressions pass, including independent Blender poses and failed-revision rollback; Lantern Post exercises real saved-source clip edits. | Revisit CPU deformation after a measured character workload justifies GPU skinning, or when another target needs a different numeric/backend contract. No cross-target performance or automatic retargeting promise. |
| D168 | The app hub is a Zinc program with Ed25519 in zinc:subtle (ZN-842, owner 2026-10-25: "au lieu de node ça peut être un serveur zinc") | **`app/hub-server` is a headless Zinc program on zinc:net; it signs its catalog with Ed25519 through `zinc:subtle` (`importKey raw/raw-private`, `generateKeyPair`, `sign`, `verify`), backed by the already-vendored Monocypher compiled into the host in its own namespace (`src/host/ed25519.cpp`), so no link line changed. The first publish of an app records its key; a retracted version keeps blocking its number; catalog and signature are rebuilt together only on publish or retract. Devices trust the app's signature, the catalog's signature (key pinned at pairing) and the SHA-256, not the server or the network.** Why: one language and one engine on both sides (owner), and the update path of `zinc publish`/`update-app` already signs with Ed25519. | Revisit when a hub must serve outside a LAN (HTTPS, `serveAsync` tls) or when the Monocypher copy in the host and in zn_tc should become one library. |
| D169 | A .zapp states what it needs, not who packed it (ZN-844, owner 2026-10-26: "rendre retro compatible et pas bloqué à la version du moteur") | **The manifest carries `needs: {zbc, rt}`; an engine runs the archive when it reads that bytecode version and has `Rt::Count >= rt` (the runtime call table only grows). The `engine` string is information; an archive without `needs` keeps the old rule (a newer packer is refused). Programs read the engine's numbers from `ZINC_ENGINE_ZBC`/`ZINC_ENGINE_RT`; the hub catalog lists each package's `zbc`/`rt` and the hub app refuses before downloading.** Rejected: semver ranges on the engine string (says nothing about what the program uses). |
| D170 | The reMarkable engine is distributed by the hub (ZN-844, owner 2026-10-26: "très bonne idée la maj du moteur depuis hub") | **`POST /publish-engine` (publisher token) stores one current bundle; the signed catalog.json carries `engine: {version, zbc, rt, url, sha256, size}`, trusted through the hub key (an engine has no publisher key). zbc/rt come from a compile-time `zinc-engine` line grepped from the cross-built binary (`zinc --engine-info`). The Hub app offers only an engine with the same zbc; it downloads beside the engine, checks SHA-256, size and the new binary's `--engine-info`, then swaps directories and keeps the old engine on any failure. A zbc change stays with deploy.sh.** |
| D171 | Ink tools and annotation formats (ZN-844, owner 2026-10-26: "une palette d'outils d'écriture comparable à xochitl ... voir si des standards existent") | **zinc:ink strokes carry a tool (ballpoint, fineliner, marker, pencil, calligraphy, highlighter; stroke and area erasers) and `InkPalette` of zinc:remarkable picks tool, colour (the Paper Pro's nine) and width. The lossless source stays zinc:ink JSON (`"tool"` additive, absent = ballpoint); a computer reads SVG per page and, for PDFs, ISO 32000 `/Ink` annotations written by PDFium with `/CA` for the highlighter. Scored (RULES.md §4): JSON+SVG+PDF 38, InkML 29, Xournal++ 29, .rm v6 22; the others wait for a user. Report: docs/reports/ink-tools-and-annotation-formats.md.** |
| D172 | Intel macOS 10.12 experiment (ZN-845, owner 2026-10-26) | **Reuse the portable console interpreter/raster with a separate Cocoa presenter, built from `targets/macos-legacy`; prepare bytecode with modern Zinc.** RULES §4 weighted scores: portable core+Cocoa 61, full CLI backport 51, portable core+SDL2 55 (details and commands in `zinc-next-macos-legacy.md`). Existing formatting fallback already handles old Apple libraries; the full CLI requires newer filesystem APIs. Intel execution/output/pixels on modern macOS under Rosetta is a first proof, not Sierra qualification. Revisit after real 10.12 execution or a concrete desktop app requirement. |
| D173 | Records across the native ABI (ZN-850, owner 2026-10-28: "le truc le plus fast, leger et parfait ... mais aussi le plus compatible et evolutif") | **A spec's interfaces cross as a binary wire on the existing B letter (PB for a promise): little-endian, no alignment and no keys; strings and byte arrays are a u32 length and bytes, lists a u32 count, optionals a 0/1 byte, a record a u32 body length then its fields in declared order. Evolution is append-only: a reader skips fields it does not know (the body length) and gives defaults for fields the writer did not have.** Native-gen writes plain std structs with encode/decode, safe on a worker; the typed engines decode through Dyn, QuickJS in one call; strict profiles refuse records (Z5010). JSON was refused as a wire (a parse and allocations at each call) and FlatBuffers/Cap'n Proto as dependencies for what fits in one header (docs/reports/native-record-wire-options-2026-10-28.md). |
| D174 | A generic class made with T = void (ZN-852, owner 2026-10-28: fill the engine's gaps) | **The checker instantiates a generic class with T = void as T = `__Void`, an empty prelude class, so `Promise<void>` from generic code (`fail<void>()`, `Promise.reject<void>`) is a real class with valid IR; where a `Promise<void>` (PromiseV) is expected, or on catch/finally, a generated helper converts it, settling one microtask later.** Merging Promise and PromiseV was not done: every prelude body and the await path assume PromiseV carries no value (backlog/later.md). |
| D175 | One Promise class (ZN-855, owner 2026-10-28: "Promise<void> et Promise<T> ne sont pas fusionnés ... then avec deux arguments est refusé") | **`Promise<void>` is `Promise<T>` with T = `__Void` (D174's rule, now the only void promise); PromiseV and its converters are gone, so a void promise settles in Node's order. The value is a plain field (`value!: T`, null for void), internal promises are made without an executor; `resolveVoid`, `__awaitV`, `__thenV`/`__catchV` stay as fast paths only. `then(f, g)` runs in one job. The checker accepts what TypeScript does around it: a trailing void parameter may be left out, a function with fewer parameters fits (an adapter), `new C()` takes its type arguments from the expected type.** Measured (M1 Pro, 1e6 awaits): an async function returning nothing 461 to 377 ms interpreted, 187 to 129 ms AOT. Left: `resolve(undefined)`, generic functions with T = void, an executor that throws, `then` flattening (backlog/later.md). |
| D176 | A documentation generator (ZN-856, owner 2026-10-29: "de manière automatique mais possible ajout manuel un peu sur le model de rust ou go ... themable et statique ... md ... et même du mdx ... export ou servir un petit serveur") | **`zinc doc` is a static site generator inside zinc: Markdown through md4c (already pinned); the API read with the frontend's lexer (depth-0 exports, signatures from the source, `/** */` or the `//` lines above, as godoc); MDX as a fixed set of components rendered at build time (Steps, Tabs, Callout, Cards, Command, Screenshot, Example, Details, ApiRef), with no JavaScript toolchain; highlighting at build time; a prebuilt search index that works from `file://`; a CSS-variable theme, light and dark, overridden by `docs/theme.css`; `--out` exports, `--serve` binds 127.0.0.1 and reloads on save. A plugin adds pages in `plugins/<name>/docs/`.** Scored 65/70 on RULES §4 against mdBook 51, MDX in QuickJS 45, VitePress/Docusaurus 36, Astro/Starlight 33, TypeDoc+Starlight 30: the only one that works on the first run, offline, on a modest machine, with nothing installed (docs/reports/zinc-doc-design-2026-10-29.md). Revisit if users need MDX expressions or their own components (rendered by Zinc), or the token reader misreads real code (a reader over the parser). |
| D177 | Duplicate tracks on an iPod (ZN-857, owner 2026-10-29: "trouver les doublons et les virer en gardant le plus complet et le meilleur, basé sur infos, fichier, emprunte") | **Audio fingerprints use Chromaprint 1.6.0 (MIT, the AcoustID reference), vendored in `plugins/ipod/vendor/chromaprint` with its bundled KissFFT (BSD-3), pinned by archive sha256, without its FFmpeg resampler (LGPL): the platform decoder (AudioToolbox ExtAudioFile on macOS) gives its 11025 Hz mono directly. Two copies are the same audio at 0.8 or more bit agreement over the first 30 s, aligned within 2 s (same melody across WAV, AAC 64 and MP3 96: about 1.0; different melodies 0.61 to 0.64). Identical files: XXH3-128, only for files whose size another shares. Fingerprints are cached on the computer beside the backups (`<backups>/<serial>/fingerprints.txt`, keyed by dbid, size and mtime): the iPod is only read. Keep score, in order: lossless, bitrate (5% tie), sample rate, tags filled, cover, track and disc numbers, year, added first.** The only proven open fingerprinter; on Linux the fingerprints wait for a decoder (backlog/later.md). |
| D178 | The iPod daemon (owner 2026-10-29: "un daemon qui tourne sur raspberrypi ou chip ntc ... piloter un ipod ... depuis une app sur le mac ... et même stream via subsonic ... ultra leger") | **`examples/ipod-web` grows into the daemon: one compiled Zinc program that owns the iPod's database and serves its page, a JSON API (`/api`, bearer token) for the Mac app's remote iPod and the Subsonic API 1.16.1 (`/rest`, XML and JSON, token auth by MD5) for any Subsonic client. Files are sent as they are with `Reply.file` (blocks, byte ranges), never transcoded. Writes need `--allow-changes`. Contract: docs/reports/ipod-daemon.md.** The engine gained what it lacked for it: `Reply.file` with ranges, zinc:net on QuickJS (rows and a loop that waits while the host listens), MD5 in `subtle.digest`. Rejected: DLNA/UPnP (heavier, fewer clients), a share of the disk (no tags, no database), MPD (plays on the Pi, not a library server), transcoding (ffmpeg on a C.H.I.P.). | Revisit when a client needs transcoding (ALAC on an old phone), or when two programs must share the iPod. |
| D179 | A value typed by an interface out of `any` (owner 2026-10-29: the engines disagreed on `const x: I = JSON.parse(text)`) | **The conversion out of `any` is a checked copy: the value holds exactly the interface's declared fields (extras dropped at every depth, a spread copies only those).** It was already the interpreter's and AOT's behaviour, it follows "conversions out of Dyn are checked" (docs/guide/02-language.md) and the fixed record layout (`Object.keys` is refused on a typed record); keeping TypeScript's extras would need a hidden Dyn bag on every record. QuickJS runs the stripped source unchecked and still keeps extras: documented in docs/guide/12-js-engines.md, its checked copy is a line of backlog/later.md. Test: tests/t0/json_typed_shape.sh. |
| D180 | Now Playing and media keys (owner 2026-10-29: "touches média et Centre de contrôle macOS") | **`zinc:system/media` with the shape of the web's Media Session API (`mediaSession.metadata = new MediaMetadata(...)`, `playbackState`, `setPositionState`, `setActionHandler`), capability `mediasession` (macOS only today, a no-op elsewhere).** It is the API people know (Electron has none; the web has navigator.mediaSession); macOS backs it with MPNowPlayingInfoCenter and MPRemoteCommandCenter. Linux (MPRIS), Windows (SMTC) and the web target follow in the cross-platform pass. Tests: system_media, macos_media. |
| D181 | Workers for compute (owner 2026-10-30: "un fil ui et un calcul") | **`zinc:worker`, the Web Workers API: a worker is an isolated program (one Machine or QuickJS runtime, its own heap and loop); messages are typed data on the D173 record wire, byte buffers transferred, nothing shared in v1; the entry is static (a literal or `new URL('…', import.meta.url)`); imports from an allowlist, plugins opt in with `"worker": true`; on profiles without threads a worker runs inline as a module with the same encode/decode.** Reference counts are not atomic, so sharing objects would crash; one wire serves threads, wasm and inline mode on all three engines. Design and order of work: docs/reports/threads-ui-compute-animation.md (proposals A-D). |
| D182 | A render thread and a compositor for animations (owner 2026-10-30: "et même un fil d'animation") | **The script stays on the OS main thread; a render thread owns GL and present (three-slot handoff in gfx.cpp); declared animations of transform, opacity and the scroll offset (timing, spring, decay, repeats) run on it without script, never layout properties (RN's native-driver error), no worklets; scroll physics and easings defined once in include/zn/scroll.h and ease.h for ui.ts and the compositor; deterministic runs and captures stay synchronous with identical goldens.** Spinners, shimmers and flings keep 60 fps while script is busy. Same report, proposals E-I; moving script off the main thread is a measured option, not a decision. |
| D183 | How zinc:worker is built (D181 steps 1 to 3, owner 2026-10-30: "après tu implement tout !") | **The runtime and host state is `thread_local` (the heap byte counter atomic); messages arrive as host-loop events (kind 70); the main side computes both record schemas from `Worker<In, Out>` and they travel with the start, the encoding matched by field name; the entry is a literal or `new URL('…', import.meta.url)`, compiled as its own program and carried in a trailing section of the ZBC module (and as its own C++ namespace by `zinc build`); `terminate()` is cooperative (next host call); a live worker keeps the loop running, and the main program's end terminates and joins them; under `ZINC_DETERMINISTIC` the main loop waits each turn until every worker is idle; native plugins are refused in a worker (v1); a moved `u8[]` is copied, then emptied; threads get an 8 MB stack (1 MB on 32-bit). QuickJS runs each worker on its own runtime and context (ZN-587, 2026-10-30): its message schemas are read from the syntax of the file and its relative imports at transform time, and a type it cannot name (a generic, an `extends`, a `zinc:` type) travels by structured clone on both sides.** Why: the Web Workers shape is what JS users know; per-thread state rather than a big lock keeps both threads at full speed; deterministic waits keep goldens stable. |
| D184 | Compile time of `zinc run` and `zinc build` (owner 2026-10-30: "améliorer le zinc run ou build pour que ça aille plus vite ... même sur machine chargé ou peu performantes") | **A whole-module check (AOT register tables, ZBC collection classes, the checker's narrowed paths) runs once per module or through an index, never once per function or per call; a plugin's sources compile `compileJobs()` at a time (`ZINC_JOBS`, else half the cores, 1 to 8), the same cap as the AOT units, without waiting for the load average.** Why: the quadratic scans were most of check, emit and AOT on the iPod demo (check 1.5 s to 0.3 s, AOT 13.7 s to 1.4 s, zinc:ipod's plugin 72 s to 19 s); waiting on the load would serialise the user's own build whenever the machine is busy, and the cap already bounds what zinc adds. |
| D185 | Threads in the profiler and the debugger (owner 2026-10-30: "on améliore le profiler et debugger pour avoir les nouvelles infos des threads") | **A trace has one named track per thread (tid 1 the main thread, 100 + id each worker, `zinc worker <entry>#<id>`) and a message is a flow s → t → f (post, receive, handler); sampling routes SIGPROF to the buffer of the thread that took it, registered through `rt::worker::machineHook` (null unless a profiler or debugger sets it); each worker is its own CDP session over the one socket (`Target.setAutoAttach`, flatten), with its own debugger on its own thread, as Chrome attaches a page's workers.** Why: the tools people know (Perfetto, speedscope, Chrome DevTools) read these shapes as they are; the hooks cost nothing when nobody listens. |
| D188 | Type-only imports and covariant arrays (engine gaps met by D181 step 4, 2026-10-30) | **Type-only imports follow TypeScript's default elision: `import type`, `{ type A }`, and a plain import whose names are read only in types (or not at all) neither load nor run their module; `import 'x'` is always kept; a class's `extends` and `typeof x` are value uses (the rule qjs/strip.cpp valueNames already applied, so the three engines agree). A module reached only that way is loaded as its types: interfaces, aliases, enums and class shapes, with no code, host imports, natives, workers or plugin; an `import type` of a function from it is refused with a message to import it as a value. `Big[]` to `Song[]` (and `Dog[]` to `Animal[]`) is accepted and makes a new array: records copied with the requested fields, objects shared, as one value converts.** Why: a worker must be able to name a plugin's types without its code; sharing the array itself needs an array reference cast the IR and AOT do not have. |
| D189 | Promise is covariant (engine gap met by D181 step 4, 2026-10-30) | **A `Promise<Sub>` where a `Promise<Base>` is expected (`Promise<R>` to `Promise<R | null>`, a record widened) becomes a new promise that adopts the first through `then`, one microtask later; where the code names the type it wants, a Promise-making generic call with no lambda argument takes the expected type when the inferred one converts to it, so `Promise.resolve(r)` returned as `Promise<R | null>` needs no conversion. A `const` (or a `let` nothing assigns later) taken from an `await` stays a const in the desugared continuation, so its narrowing survives later awaits. `__adopt<T>` subscribes its own job instead of going through `__await`, so `Promise<void>.then(async ...)` adopts.** Why: TypeScript's typing of promises; each Promise instance is its own class and the IR has no reference cast, as for D188's arrays. |
| D190 | Type-only import cycles and conditional unions (engine gaps, 2026-10-30) | **The checker works per strongly connected component of the import graph (dependencies first): each module of a component binds its outside imports, hoists its type names and publishes a partial export table, then the component's shapes are linked and built, then each body is checked; value cycles stay refused by the loader. A conditional with branches of different types is the union of its branches as in TypeScript; two promises join to the promise of the union (`Promise<null> | Promise<R>` is `Promise<R | null>`), since a union of promises could not be awaited; inside `console.log` two primitive branches print their own text.** Why: shared `types.ts` cycles are common in TypeScript apps. A union of primitives still has no IR type (Z0005), see backlog/later.md. |
| D191 | Worker messages straight from typed objects (iPod model hand-off froze the UI ~0.3 s, 2026-10-30) | **`zinc:worker` encodes and decodes from the program's objects: the lowering writes the message type's layout (`recLayout`) next to the D173 schema, the runtime matches them by field name once per program and caches the plan on the Machine; no Dyn view, no JSON, no `u8[]` in between. The D173 wire and subset receivers are unchanged; `self.postMessage(v)` becomes `__workerPost<T>(v)`; the old host rows stay in the table, unserved, so row numbers do not shift.** Why: the old path cost ~1 µs per record on each side (100k records: ~100 ms); now ~4 ms to post and ~7 ms to receive. |
| D192 | A class instance where a field-only interface is expected (engine gap, 2026-10-30) | **The checker keeps a field-only interface a record and accepts a class whose public, non-getter fields match each record field with the identical type (also in arrays, parameters, returns, destructuring); it records the pair, and only such "open" records become an IR interface that the classes and a concrete literal class implement: values are shared through RefCast with no copy, and each field access tests `InstOf` per class, literal class first (closed world). Records nobody assigns a class to keep direct field access. `any` back to a base class finds subclasses of later modules through a `__dynDown` stub resolved once every module is checked, as `__dynSub`.** Why: TypeScript shares the object, so a copy would lose writes; turning every implemented record into an interface would cost a virtual call per read and drop JSON, worker and literal features. Limits kept: identical field types, a class source only, no record to class. |
| D193 | Open records, wider (follows D192; engine gaps, 2026-10-30) | **A record field is supplied by a public field or a getter of a class or a non-record interface whose type is assignable (literal, union member, subclass, machine number); a missing field is allowed only when optional; an interface extending an imported record is a record. Open-record accesses keep the D192 `InstOf` chain, extended: interfaces expand to their implementations, getters are virtual calls, writes go to setters, an impossible read or write throws a TypeError with JavaScript's message. `iface.prop = v` adds a synthetic `__set_prop` selector on first write only (no larger vtables otherwise; `readonly` still refused). Printing, JSON and `any` of records route by runtime class through stubs resolved after checking (`__fmtSub`, `__jsSub`, `__dynSub`, `__dynDown`), a constant null when no class is shared as the record. The inliner no longer inlines functions of one call-graph cycle (Tarjan).** Why: TypeScript's structural rules share the object; routing by class after all modules are checked keeps closed-world dispatch cheap and leaves untouched records with direct field access; the inliner unrolled mutual recursion until it ran out of registers. |
| D194 | The Zinc shell on the Raspberry Pi (owner 2026-10-30: "un shell zinc ultra simple et efficace ... compatible du pi1 au pi5", a top bar always shown whatever the app) | **The player is the shell: a Zinc program on display-fbdev (DRM master, evdev) with a `zinc:shell` plugin that listens on `ZINC_SHELL_SOCKET`, starts apps as the agent does (pid and log files) and shows one app below its bar; apps draw through a `display-shell` driver into three buffers the shell gives them (dmabuf of dumb buffers, shm otherwise), over `remote_proto.h`'s framing with local types (SH_*), and the shell puts them on a KMS plane (P2) or composes them in software (P1). GPU apps take the screen under the existing lease; an app that does not connect within 3 s is given the screen. The store is `app/hub-server` with a target per package.** Scored (RULES §4): player-as-shell 64, a native C++ compositor 57, a system bar inside every app 59, cage/labwc with Wayland clients 30; dumb buffers + dmabuf 65, shm and blit 58; the remote_proto framing 69, Wayland protocol 45 (docs/reports/zinc-shell/03-design.md, phases P0-P5). | Revisit if the shell's frame loop adds more than one refresh of latency (R5, measured in P2), or when two apps must share the screen. |
| D195 | Whole-system (L1) updates of the Pi image (owner 2026-10-30: "le système de mise à jour / firmware"; `ota-update-research-2026-10-27.md` §6: RAUC 25, own updater 18) | **RAUC 1.13 (Debian `rauc`, `rauc-service`) with a custom bootloader backend (`/usr/lib/zinc/zinc-ab-boot`); the slot switch is the firmware's `os_prefix=a/\|b/` in `config.txt` plus a one-shot `tryboot.txt` on one shared boot FAT (Ubuntu's piboot-ab scheme: Pi 3, Zero 2, 4, 5; arm64 images).** Layout p1 boot FAT (shared, not A/B), p2 slot A, p3 slot B of equal size, the card not grown; no data partition: post-install copies accounts, network, SSH, `/home`, `/var/lib/zinc` and enabled services into the new slot. Each rootfs carries its boot files in `/usr/lib/zinc/boot`. Trust: the Ed25519 update key (`zinc verify`, `.raucb.sig`) before RAUC's X.509 keyring (`tools/pi-image keys`). Health: `zinc-shell` active 30 s without restart within 180 s marks good, else mark-bad and reboot; `panic=10` and the watchdog cover hangs. Opt-in `"ab": true`; the single-slot image stays the default. Not used: `autoboot.txt` (Pi 4/5 EEPROM only), U-Boot (Pi 1 and Zero). |
| D196 | WebSocket server framing in zinc:net (ZN-893) | **wslay 1.1.1 (MIT, nghttp2 project) driven by our libuv loop and send path, so wss and drain come for free; one native WebSocket server in zinc:net, wrapped by zinc:socket `serveWebSocket` (the Zinc framing code stays for the client).** uWebSockets rejected: its own TLS stack beside mbedTLS and its own loop beside libuv. | Revisit if memory per idle connection matters (two 4 KiB wslay buffers each) or permessage-deflate is needed. |
| D197 | Certificates of a self-hosted zinc:net server (ZN-893) | **ACME http-01 written in Zinc (`zinc:net/acme`, JSON over fetch) plus three mbedTLS crypto ops; a renewed certificate hot-swaps the listener's TLS context (`setCertificate`), open connections keep the old one; the default bind stays IPv4 `0.0.0.0` (on macOS a `::` bind succeeds even when another program holds the IPv4 port).** No ACME library fits C++ and mbedTLS. | Revisit for dns-01 (wildcards) or dual-stack by default. |
| D198 | Self-hosted call server (owner 2026-10-31: "selhost un serveur ... appel webrtc optimisé même en faible condition", share links between Zinc apps, the web and iOS; `webrtc-selfhost-design.md`) | **Hybrid: Galène 1.2.1 (MIT, Go on Pion) carries the media (SFU, simulcast, NACK, TURN, invite tokens); Zinc owns everything around it (`examples/webrtc-call/server`: pinned binary, config, ACME, share links with QR, systemd unit); browsers use Galène's client, Zinc apps speak its protocol through zinc:socket and zinc:webrtc.** Scored 59/70 against LiveKit 55, mesh 55, mediasoup 45, an SFU in Zinc 42, Janus 42, Jitsi 38. | Revisit for LiveKit if TURN over TLS 443, transport-cc, RED or several nodes are needed; only the Zinc client would change. |
| D199 | Text input of a code editor on the web (ZN-908, owner: "un mode textarea, canvas, ou un div contenteditable") | **Zinc keeps drawing the editor; one hidden `<textarea>` at the caret holds the focus and carries keys, text, IME, dead keys and paste into zinc:ui's own editing; pointer and wheel go to zinc:ui; no focus ring** (Monaco and Google Docs; Zinc's canvas mode already did this). In DOM render only code fields take this path; other text fields stay native. | Revisit if screen readers need the code text in the DOM. |
| D200 | Echo cancellation, noise suppression and gain of the microphone (ZN-871) | **SpeexDSP 1.2.1 (BSD, +84 KB) behind `plugins/microphone/native/voice.h`**, scored 56 against webrtc-audio-processing (AEC3, NS, AGC2) 52: size, portability (C, Pi and ESP32 class) and effort win over AEC3's better delay estimation. Measured: ERLE 25.8 dB in a simulated room. | Revisit if real calls show residual echo or clipped double talk, or a speaker-to-microphone delay above 250 ms (Bluetooth): AEC3 and AGC2 can replace it behind `voice.h`. |
| D201 | Video congestion control of zinc:webrtc (ZN-901) | **Receiver: NACK every round trip (10 tries or 1 s) then PLI at most once a second; a port of libwebrtc's trendline estimator drives REMB (not the Kalman filter, which gave false over/underuse on a jittery link); recovery climbs 1.25 per second back to the 60 s peak. Sender: min(REMB, loss) with the ladder 720p30, 360p30, 360p15, 180p15, 90p7, off. Loss signal is max(receiver report fraction, unique NACKed numbers over packets sent); the ladder steps down only when the limiting source just fell and the VP8 QP is over 60 or unknown.** | Revisit with transport-cc and an acked bitrate: REMB alone still freezes the picture 2-4 s at a big rate step. |
| D202 | VP8 layers and simulcast of zinc:webrtc (ZN-902) | **Our own VP8 packetizer in `plugins/webrtc/vendor/capi_zinc.cpp` (libdatachannel's `capi.cpp` stays unmodified) writes the full descriptor (15-bit PictureID, TL0PICIDX, TID, Y; N only on frames nothing refers to); L1T2 by default; two rid encodings go out only when the answer has `a=simulcast:recv` and the rid extension.** VP8 only; a Zinc peer takes one SSRC. | Revisit for H.264 simulcast and Zinc-to-Zinc rid routing. |
| D204 | What `zinc run` and `zinc dev` print (owner 2026-11-03: "remettre ce qu'il se passe lors de run, dev, comme dans le build"; reverses the single progress line of ZN-774.32) | **Every step with its time, in English, on stderr, as `zinc build` prints them: the common base (engine, cache, compile) and what each action does (watch, change, swap, compile of the save, resources, check).** One line hid where the time goes on a modest machine, and a first run looked frozen. `-q` or `ZINC_STEPS=0` silences them, and tests run with `ZINC_STEPS=0` and opt in to assert them. |
| D205 | Files, demo bar and fetch proxy of an exported web page (ZN-863.02, .05, .07) | **`zinc:fs` in the page keeps files in memory (synchronous calls) and copies them to IndexedDB (`zinc-fs:<app id>`) after each change and when the page is hidden; the project's files listed in `targets.web.files` ship beside the page as a read-only layer, media streamed. The page settings live under `targets.web` (`files`, `demo`, `proxy`, `proxyUrl`). A demo bar names what the page cannot run; missing parts still throw ZnUnsupported. The proxy is a host allow-list for GET and HEAD only (`/__proxy?url=`, no credentials, no private address by name, 32 MB, 20 s), served by `serve.py` and exported as `proxy.mjs` for Workers or Deno.** | OPFS's synchronous handles work only in a worker and older Safari lacks its main-thread writer; Emscripten IDBFS and Pyodide use the same memory-plus-IndexedDB pattern; no `navigator.storage.persist()` (Firefox prompts). The top-level `web` key already means the QuickJS web environment. | Apps that write thousands of files (a folder index), a DNS rebinding concern (pin the resolved address), a browser that gives the main thread a synchronous OPFS |
| D206 | When `app.id` is required (ZN-799) | **Only where an app is packaged or signed (`zinc build --bundle`, `--dmg`, the store manifests); `app.window` and a plain run need none, iOS and Android manifests fall back to `dev.zinc.<name>`; a malformed id is still refused. `lib/ui.d.ts` is generated from `lib/std/ui.ts` by `tools/ui-dts` and committed; `ui_dts` fails when it is stale.** | Tauri and Electron ask for an identifier only to package; a first project should run with no id. | A platform API that needs an id at run time |
| D207 | Text fallback on by default (ZN-1006, revises D32's default) | **The shaping tier (HarfBuzz, the fallback chain: embedded fonts, then Apple Color Emoji, Apple Symbols, Arial Unicode; Noto paths on Linux) is on wherever it is linked: `zinc run` on the three engines, the compiled run, and a host `zinc build` that links `libzn_text_gfx.a`. `"text": "simple"` in zinc.json or `ZINC_TEXT=simple` opts out; `"shaped"` still forces it. App libraries and the Android export link it only on request. | D208 | Machine-readable output and failed native plugin builds (ZN-1021, ZN-1027, ZN-1044) | **JSON output goes to stdout only and carries a `format` number; keys may be added, never renamed (`zinc project --json`, `zinc plugins --json`); `zinc check --json` stays a bare array for the LSP. A native plugin that fails to build stops `zinc pack` and `zinc export` (`ZINC_NATIVE_REQUIRED=1`), with the whole compiler error; the stand-in is opt-in with `ZINC_NATIVE=sim`, and `zinc run` still falls back with one visible line. Packages ship the sources of plugins that take options, beside their prebuilt library.** | Tools (Atelier, editors) must stop parsing text; npm, pip, cargo and Flutter fail a build whose native part fails instead of shipping a silent stand-in; the index's binaries are keyed by the sources, so an install without them cannot match a key. | Build steps as JSON lines, plugin options passed at load time instead of as compile-time defines |
| D209 | QuickJS agrees with the typed engines on data (ZN-1009, ZN-1008, ZN-1011) | **`JSON.parse` at a typed declaration or an `as` is a checked copy on QuickJS too (`__dynAs` with a syntactic schema: declared fields kept, i32 truncated, the same TypeError on a missing field); other conversion sites wait for the checker's types in the transform (ZN-796). A plugin's `u8[]` result crosses to the JS engines as a plain array up to 64 Ki elements and above that as a typed array whose `map`, `concat` and `toJSON` act as an array's. `@async/` AsyncStorage keys are copied once into each app's own store (marked by `storage.kv.migrated`), never moved.** | Programs must give the same results on the three engines; a plain array costs about 16 ms per million elements on QuickJS against 2 ms for a typed one, so camera and screen frames stay typed; copying shared keys loses no data. | `push` on a large native result, a QuickJS fast path for arrays from natives, the checker's types reaching the transform |
| D210 | Real AppKit views for macOS apps, the React Native way (owner approved 2026-11-08; ZN-1069 to ZN-1076) | **A second renderer of the zinc:ui tree, chosen per project (`"ui": { "render": "native" }`): zinc:ui keeps its tree, its models and its Yoga layout (text and controls measured by AppKit); after each changed frame a host plugin applies one buffer of changes to AppKit views on the main thread, with events coming back through the functions the web's semantic DOM already uses. `zinc:appkit` adds typed components for controls with no primitive (window, toolbar, split view and sidebar, table and outline, buttons, pickers, popover, sheet), each with a drawn fallback; custom native views come through plugins with generated glue. No general Objective-C bridge, no SwiftUI hosting. The drawn UI stays the default everywhere; D59 stands.** | Scored (RULES §4, max 70): native host of the zinc:ui tree 58, a separate AppKit reconciler 52, SwiftUI hosting 47, a general objc bridge 36. It reuses the DOM host's shape and Yoga, and keeps programs portable. Report: `docs/reports/appkit-native-ui-research.md`. | The zinc:ui walk dominating the commit (then a direct node store), two hosts (AppKit, UIKit) costing more than one Catalyst host, a stable generated Apple binding making an expert `zinc:objc` bridge cheap |
| D211 | Auto-import in zinc lsp (ZN-1020) | **Auto-import edits ride in the completion items themselves (`additionalTextEdits`), not through `completionItem/resolve`; `sys.kill(pid, '0')` tests that a process exists, as in Node (EPERM counts as alive).** | Zinc's lists are small and filtered by prefix; tsserver and rust-analyzer resolve lazily only because their indexes are large. One request less keeps Atelier simple. | Completion lists large enough that building every edit shows in the latency |
| D212 | What an export ships (ZN-797, ZN-816, ZN-817) | **Exports and packs always ship the native plugin code (`ZINC_NATIVE_REQUIRED` overrides `ZINC_DETERMINISTIC`). A web part that is not available throws `ZnUnsupported`, never a plain Error (apps check it with `instanceof`). Source maps carry project-relative `sources` with `sourceRoot` and `sourcesContent`, never paths of the build machine. `.qbc` files carry the engine version and a mismatched one is never read. The V8 pack is pinned by bottle sha; a bump is a commit of its own.** | A shipped app must not carry test fakes, must fail quietly where a platform lacks a part, must trace errors on any machine, and must not run bytecode of another engine build. | A stable QuickJS bytecode format across versions; an engine pack source other than Homebrew |
| D213 | Runtime: output order, string indexes, real-time headless (ZN-643, ZN-1015, ZN-1005) | **The program's output is flushed before a child process starts and before stdin is read, on every engine, from one list (`zn::flushesOutBefore`), still not before every host call (D65 stands). A non-ASCII string keeps a UTF-16 cursor (last index read) in `StrObj` padding, so forward loops are linear; no lock, since workers get copies. Headless runs pace frames at 60 a second only with `ZINC_REALTIME=1` (`ZINC_DURATION=ms` ends the run); the default stays as fast as possible.** | Output must not come after a child's or interleave with a prompt; a cursor costs no memory where a full UTF-16 copy doubles it; benchmarks and captures rely on unpaced headless runs. | Random-access patterns that defeat the cursor (then index markers); a `stdio: 'inherit'` child option |
| D214 | Lessons run in their page; lint in the editor (ZN-774.25, ZN-1063) | **A docs lesson runs from the web export the gallery already commits (`site/public/play/<lesson>/`, about 40 KB), not from `.zbc` on `vm-web.wasm`: no 1.6 MB wasm, no COOP/COEP worker; browsers without import maps keep the picture and the `zinc run` line. `zinc lsp` runs `zinc lint --json` on open and save for a project that names targets and shows the target findings as diagnostics.** | The exports exist and are small; target findings are worth seeing where the code is written. | A lesson needing a native side the web lacks; lint on save stalling large projects (then background or debounce) |
| D215 | Methods on `any`, generic `instanceof`, closing windows (ZN-1035, ZN-389, ZN-1032) | **A method call on an `any` is allowed when its name is an array or string method, as in TypeScript; it runs on the value itself (a change is seen through the `any`), only the value's kind is checked at run time, and every other method stays Z0105. Records declared first convert to a wider record type by a copy at the call. `instanceof` with a generic class narrows a union to the one instance of that class it holds (none or several is an error that says so). No app-level window-all-closed hook: the program ends with its last window (Electron's default) and a per-window `onClosed` plus `quit` ends it with the main window; window ids are never reused.** | TypeScript's behaviour on `any`; the copy already worked for read-only props; Electron's model for windows without a second lifecycle. | Writes through a converted array that must be seen in the original; tray apps that live with no window |
| D216 | npm packages, one clock, canvas alpha, DevTools names (ZN-815, ZN-708, ZN-1064, ZN-812) | **The JavaScript engines resolve npm packages as a browser build (the `browser` condition and field first, as esbuild, Vite and webpack do for a browser target) and give packages the common Web globals (`zinc:web/globals`, never `document`/`window`/`navigator`). `Date.now` and `new Date()` read one clock on every engine: the wall clock in a normal run, Unix 0 in UTC (unless TZ is set) moving with the virtual clock in a deterministic run. A fading canvas multiplies its alpha into each primitive (`setGlobalAlpha`), as Android does for views without overlapping rendering, not an offscreen layer. DevTools host nodes are named as JSX writes them (`View`, `className`); a component wrapper has an empty `localName`.** | QuickJS has no Node built-ins (nanoid's and qs's Node builds fail); results must not depend on the day; no image per fading canvas; Chrome shows the tree as written. | A browser build needing `window`/`document`; a library refusing 1970 dates; overlapping strokes showing through in a fading canvas |
| D217 | GL replay atlas and driver quirks (ZN-182, ZN-183) | **The glyph atlas has up to 4 pages created as needed and evicts the least recently used whole page, always flushing the pending batch before overwriting it (Skia's GrDrawOpAtlas rules at page level). Driver quirks (`no-replay`, `no-gpu-poly`, `mask-clip`, `clip-depth`) are bits of the capability record set from a table keyed on GPU family and renderer string, overridable with `ZINC_GL_QUIRK`; `no-replay` makes every host draw with the software raster after one line. No software renderer gets `no-replay` from the table.** | One reset of a full atlas redrew every glyph; quirks as capability bits are testable without GL (D119); `gl_renderer.sh` runs the replay on llvmpipe. | Apps mixing many sizes or colour fonts (plots inside pages, RGBA pages); a startup self-check that sets `no-replay` on a driver that draws wrong |
| D218 | Dev loop red box; native build in the background (ZN-1024, ZN-1026; revises D43) | **On a compile error in `zinc dev` the program keeps running with its state under the red box, cleared at the next successful swap. `zinc run` of a program whose native build is missing or stale runs on the interpreter at once and builds it in a detached, niced builder for the next run; `--native` waits for the build; a failed build is remembered beside the run cache, keyed by the toolchain, until that changes. Coordinator guard: headless and deterministic runs (tests, agents, scripts) still build in the foreground, and a machine-wide lock (`cache/run/background`) allows one background builder at a time. Windows keeps the foreground build.** | A window in seconds on the first run; D43's speed from the second run; no detached compilers piling up on the 16 GB machine. | A run whose sources changed but whose C++ key did not is now interpreted once; the builder has no memory cap yet |
| D219 | Canvas patterns, Web Audio oscillators, gamepad mappings (ZN-1042, ZN-1043) | **Patterns are sampled as Chrome's default `imageSmoothingQuality` "low": nearest or bilinear, never bicubic.
